## 1. Introduction

Recent frontier lar<sub>g</sub>e lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e models (LLMs), includin<sub>g</sub> GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5.1, su<sub>pp</sub>ort com<sub>p</sub>lex reasonin<sub>g</sub>, codin<sub>g</sub> and lon<sub>g</sub>-horizon a<sub>g</sub>entic work (O<sub>p</sub>enAI, 2026; Anthro<sub>p</sub>ic, 2026). These ca<sub>p</sub>abilities renew interest in how far lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-model s<sub>y</sub>stems can <sub>g</sub>eneralize be<sub>y</sub>ond their trainin<sub>g</sub> tasks (Fen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2024). O<sub>p</sub>en-wei<sub>g</sub>ht Qwen3.8-Flash Next, Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek-V4.1-Flash and Kimi K3 broaden access to ca<sub>p</sub>able a<sub>g</sub>ent models (Qwen Team, 2026; Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek-AI, 2026; Moonshot AI, 2026).

These advances extend lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-model ca<sub>p</sub>abilities (Zhao et al., 2026) to interactive software and web tasks (Jimenez et al., 2024; Yao et al., 2022). A<sub>g</sub>ent harnesses connect these models to executable tools and <sub>p</sub>ersistent state. O<sub>p</sub>enHands <sub>p</sub>rovides a com<sub>p</sub>osable software-a<sub>g</sub>ent SDK (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026c); <sub>π</sub> a<sub>g</sub>ent combines a terminal a<sub>g</sub>ent loo<sub>p</sub> with extensible tools, model-<sub>p</sub>rovider interfaces and resumable sessions (Earendil Works, 2026). Throu<sub>g</sub>h these s<sub>y</sub>stems, a<sub>g</sub>ents ins<sub>p</sub>ect re<sub>p</sub>ositories, run commands and revise solutions usin<sub>g</sub> tool feedback (Qin et al., 2024; Liu et al., 2025). How should we analyze and transform failed agent experience so that models can learn to recognize, avoid, and repair errors across execution settings?

Existin<sub>g</sub> work has advanced several as<sub>p</sub>ects of learnin<sub>g</sub> from a<sub>g</sub>ent failures. MAST (Cemri et al., 2026b) characterizes failure modes, and Who&When (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025b) identifies res<sub>p</sub>onsible a<sub>g</sub>ents and decisive error ste<sub>p</sub>s. These <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons suppor</sub>t f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t d<sub>o no</sub>t di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y spec</sub>if<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>ons an agen</sub>t <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld l<sub>earn</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>.</sub> A<sub>gen</sub> Tracer (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a) <sub>g</sub>oes further b<sub>y</sub> constructin<sub>g</sub> attribution data and trainin<sub>g</sub> a dia<sub>g</sub>nostic model, while A<sub>g</sub>entDebu<sub>g</sub> (Zhu et al., 2025) uses corrective feedback to recover from failures. Their <sub>p</sub>rinci<sub>p</sub>al learnin<sub>g</sub> and recover<sub>y</sub> objectives, however, difer from training an acting policy on corrected behavior. Across these resources, diferences i<sub>n execu</sub>ti<sub>on se</sub>tti<sub>n s, anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>ar e</sub>t<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>on ev</sub>id<sub>ence a</sub>l<sub>so ma</sub>k<sub>e</sub> it difi<sub>cu</sub>lt t<sub>o s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d di<sub>a nos</sub>i<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng on a common co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ures.</sub> A b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>er resource</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore nee</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o connec</sub>t <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ures w</sub>ith <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>ons, propose</sub>d <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>ons, an</sub>d <sub>execu</sub>ti<sub>on ev</sub>id<sub>ence, an</sub>d t<sub>o suppor</sub>t t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>or</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

We introduce the Agent Error Dataset (AED), a collection of 50,228 error–diagnosis pairs spanning 33 environments, 19 harness families and 23 policy models. This breadth supports failure analysis across text-based execution settings; stored traces also su<sub>pp</sub>ort re-dia<sub>g</sub>nosis without new rollouts. Our A<sub>g</sub>entic Error-to-Trainin<sub>g</sub> (AET) <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline links diagnoses and proposed corrections to matched replay outcomes where supported, then constructs objective-specific trainin<sub>g</sub> views (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 2). The collection counts <sub>p</sub>airs, while ex<sub>p</sub>eriments use a se<sub>p</sub>aratel<sub>y</sub> frozen dia<sub>g</sub>nosis release and distinct re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> and actor cohorts (Section 3.3).

Our contributions connect failure analysis, correction testing and post-training. (i) A scaled collection of natural agent failures retains provenance across execution settings, supporting analysis of how failures vary with the model and harness (Section 3.3). (ii) AET, a pipeline for reusing failed experience, links diagnoses, corrections and optional controlled replay to separate training views. First-proposal corrections improve matched-replay pass rates by 32.7 percentage points over original-action retries (Section 5.1). (iii) An empirical study of error-aware post-training s<sup>h</sup>ows t<sup>h</sup>at <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup>-diagnosis SFT on 1,656 source tas<sup>k</sup>s raises interna<sup>l</sup> exact-step teac<sup>h</sup>er-<sup>l</sup>a<sup>b</sup>e<sup>l</sup> agreement <sup>f</sup>rom 47.2% to 63.6% across three seeds.

## 2. Related Work

Attribution and failure analysis. Understanding failures requires connecting recurring error patterns to decisions within individual runs. MAST and AdaMAST or<sub>g</sub>anize these <sub>p</sub>atterns into taxonomies (Cemri et al., 2026b;a), while attribution researchers identif<sub>y</sub> res<sub>p</sub>onsible a<sub>g</sub>ents and error locations (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025b; Desh<sub>p</sub>ande et al., 2025; Barke et al., 2026), includin<sub>g</sub> in lon<sub>g</sub> traces and s<sub>p</sub>ans (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a;b; Chen et al., 2026; Xia et al., 2026).

![](images/c07c918df9fb6f66e2380db5f4ee2d1270a2791a5765a3126bd8173b5165a5f8.jpg)  
Figure 2: AET construction and training views. Natural failures yield diagnoses, corrections and optional replay evidence. Coverage, <sup>h</sup>istorica<sup>l</sup> error pro<sup>fil</sup>es and experiments use separate co<sup>h</sup>orts. Rep<sup>l</sup>ay <sup>b</sup>ars use <sup>fi</sup>rst/se<sup>l</sup>ected proposa<sup>l</sup>s (n = 3,062 eac<sup>h</sup>) and restored-<sup>h</sup>arness se<sup>l</sup>ected proposa<sup>l</sup>s (n = 871), eac<sup>h</sup> wit<sup>h</sup> its own contro<sup>l</sup>; t<sup>h</sup>ey measure correction uti<sup>l</sup>ity. “Step DPO” denotes an ofline action-<sub>p</sub>reference <sub>p</sub>ilot (A<sub>pp</sub>endix D.1).

To scale attribution supervision, AgenTracer trains localization models, Who&When Pro expands fault injection, and AEGIS verifies injected failures before trainin<sub>g</sub> attribution (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a; Liu et al., 2026; Kon<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). For recovery, the question extends to whether a diagnosed error persists and whether a correction helps: TrajDebug tracks error resolution and introduces TrajErrBench (Qi et al., 2026), while A<sub>g</sub>entDebu<sub>g</sub> and A<sub>g</sub>entDebu<sub>g</sub>X connect dia<sub>g</sub>nosis to corrective execution (Zhu et al., 2025; 2026); CUADebu<sub>g</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>lies dia<sub>g</sub>nosis and re<sub>p</sub>air to screenshot-based com<sub>p</sub>uter use (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026b). In AED, we use ex<sub>p</sub>lorator<sub>y</sub> error <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub>s for cross-settin<sub>g</sub> anal<sub>y</sub>sis, and link natural failures to dia<sub>g</sub>noses, <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osed corrections and available re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> evidence to su<sub>pp</sub>ort <sub>p</sub>ost-trainin<sub>g</sub> (Table 1).

Learning from unsuccessful experience. Prior work reuses failed actions, segments and goals (Lan et al., 2025; Din<sub>g</sub>, 2026; Li et al., 2026; Yin et al., 2026), constructs reflective recover<sub>y</sub> trajectories (Yuan et al., 2025; Chen et al., 2025; Kruen<sub>g</sub>krai & Yoshino, 2025), and <sub>g</sub>uides later behavior with criti<sub>q</sub>ues (Shinn et al., 2023; Kumar et al., 2025). Other resources retain executable environments and verifiers (Xu et al., 2025; Pan et al., 2024; Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026); ADP unifies trajector<sub>y</sub> formats (Son<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). On a se<sub>p</sub>arate re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-su<sub>pp</sub>orted subset, we com<sub>p</sub>are corrections with same-check<sub>p</sub>oint ori<sub>g</sub>inal-action retries (Table 1). A<sub>pp</sub>endix C.3 details resource sco<sub>p</sub>es; A<sub>pp</sub>endix T.3 tests trajector<sub>y</sub> <sub>expor</sub>t<sub>, no</sub>t f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>-recor</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>eropera</sub>bilit<sub>y.</sub>

## 3.2. AET: a five-stage data generation pipeline

AET connects five stages (Figure 2). (1) Collect natural failures across environments, harnesses and policies, preserving action–observation traces and screening out known infrastructure or grader faults from agent-error targets. (2) Diagnose the error location and responsible agent, with a trace-cited explanation and proposed correction, following AgentDebug (Zhu et al., 2025) through AgentDebugX (Zhu et al., 2026). (3) Ground the diagnosis in the student-visible trace through structural and semantic checks, recording accepted and rejected proposals. (4) Replay, where supported, t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e correc</sub>ti<sub>on aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>a</sub> f<sub>res</sub>h <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l<sub>-ac</sub>ti<sub>on re</sub>t<sub>ry</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e same c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy,</sub> h<sub>arness,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ver</sub>ifi<sub>er se</sub>tti<sub>ngs.</sub> B<sub>o</sub>th <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>comes are re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>; recovery suppor</sub>t<sub>s a correc</sub>ti<sub>on un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>ose con</sub>diti<sub>ons,</sub> without establishing a unique root cause. (5) Build views for diagnosis SFT, recovery SFT and action preferences, each with its own evidence and s<sub>p</sub>lit re<sub>q</sub>uirements (Section 4). Dia<sub>g</sub>nosis records re<sub>q</sub>uire no re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>; recover<sub>y</sub> tar<sub>g</sub>ets <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re an execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, pass</sub>i<sub>ng con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>f<sub>erences requ</sub>i<sub>re a va</sub>lid <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d<sub>-</sub>i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>son.</sub> St<sub>ore</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ures</sub> <sub>can rece</sub>i<sub>ve a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l di<sub>agnoses w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>new ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s, w</sub>hil<sub>e recor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t f<sub>a</sub>il <sub>a v</sub>i<sub>ew</sub>’<sub>s a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>on ru</sub>l<sub>es rema</sub>i<sub>n ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>or co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on au</sub>dit<sub>s.</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> D<sub>.</sub>1 <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>age con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> E<sub>.</sub>6 th<sub>e rev</sub>i<sub>ew ru</sub>b<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c.</sub>

Takeaway: Exploratory error profiles difer across harnesses

Scope of the reported evidence
<table><tr><td>Scope</td><td>Interpretation of the measured results</td></tr><tr><td>Label validity</td><td>Quotation matching checks presence, not entailment or a unique cause. Teacher references and reconstructed traces require student-visibility audits. The assisted human audit finds higher agreement on error location than on diagnosis acceptance; its coverage-selected</td></tr><tr><td>Construction benefit</td><td>sample cannot estimate collection-wide accuracy. Paired replay tests correction utility, not attribution accuracy. Production yield and historical student comparisons differ in teacher access or task selection and do not isolate pipeline label quality.</td></tr><tr><td>Coverage and error rates</td><td>Failure-only samples cannot estimate deployment failure rates. Unequal task and model mixtures confound harness rankings and model-version trends. Text-only coverage, sparse coding cells and procedural stand-ins limit generality; environment counts are not counts of public benchmarks.</td></tr><tr><td>Diagnostic transfer</td><td>Internal holdouts share environments and often harnesses and policies with training. Public comparisons depend on reference access, prompts, coordinates and budgets. Localization on known failures does not test general failure detection, and current comparisons do not</td></tr><tr><td>Learned repair</td><td>establish public SOTA. Paired replay measures an intervention outcome, not learned autonomous recovery. Actor gains vary by environment, and the single-seed recipe comparisons also differ in exposure and update count. The action-DPO pilot measures offline preferences rather than task recovery.</td></tr></table>

## B.2. Repair outcomes and diagnostic revision

MAST distin<sub>g</sub>uishes failure <sub>p</sub>rofiles from s<sub>y</sub>stem rankin<sub>g</sub>s and relates failure modes to task outcomes (Cemri et al., 2026b). We extend this anal<sub>y</sub>sis <sub>p</sub>ers<sub>p</sub>ective to two <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>erties recorded b<sub>y</sub> AED: what ha<sub>pp</sub>ens when an action chan<sub>g</sub>es, <sub>an</sub>d h<sub>ow</sub> th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>s c</sub>h<sub>anges a</sub>ft<sub>er an unsuccess</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e co</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>s</sub> b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>on ru</sub>l<sub>es;</sub> neither estimates <sub>p</sub>revalence in the final collection. A<sub>g</sub>entDebu<sub>g</sub> motivates dia<sub>g</sub>nosis-<sub>g</sub>uided re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> (Zhu et al., 2025); thi<sub>s ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s exam</sub>i<sub>nes</sub> th<sub>e recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>comes, no</sub>t <sub>a new repa</sub>i<sub>r a</sub>l<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>m.</sub>

Successful correction alone does not isolate corrective benefit. The first proposal succeeds on 1,564 paired attempts, <sup>b</sup>ut t<sup>h</sup>e origina<sup>l</sup>-action retry a<sup>l</sup>so succeeds on 470 o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>em (30.1%). ## Takeaway: Separate a passing branch from a useful correction

O<sub>u</sub>t<sub>come-on</sub>l<sub>y recor</sub>d<sub>s canno</sub>t di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h <sub>success a</sub>l<sub>so reac</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y re</sub>t<sub>ry</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Fi<sub>na</sub>l<sub>-</sub>di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>s-on</sub>l<sub>y recor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> hid<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e rev</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ons use</sub>d t<sub>o reac</sub>h th<sub>a</sub>t b<sub>ranc</sub>h<sub>.</sub> AED <sub>preserves</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th<sub>, ena</sub>bli<sub>ng ana</sub>l<sub>yses o</sub>f <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>on u</sub>tilit<sub>y an</sub>d h<sub>ypo</sub>th<sub>es</sub>i<sub>s rev</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>s.</sub> T<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>ene</sub>fit<sub>s requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub> th<sub>e separa</sub>t<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>sons</sub> i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 5<sub>.</sub>

Success, symptoms and semantic labels measure diferent things. In MAST, some annotated failure modes also occur in successful traces. Our successful-trajectory prompt control likewise changes error-reporting behavior when the <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t <sub>p</sub>ermits abstention (A<sub>pp</sub>endix L). Neither task success nor an error claim <sub>p</sub>rovides a semantic correctness label by itself. We therefore keep symptom counts, judge-assigned categories and executed outcomes separate. The labeling-method study in Appendix G reports judge disagreement; it does not estimate error frequencies in the full <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

## D. Construction, Replay, and Evidence

Implementation and prior work. AgentDebug (Zhu et al., 2025) motivates diagnosis-guided recovery. Our diagnosis brid<sub>g</sub>e calls A<sub>g</sub>entDebu<sub>g</sub>X’s P<sub>y</sub>thon attribution API (Zhu et al., 2026) for action blame, evidence and <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osed corrections. AED <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>es env</sub>i<sub>ronmen</sub>t <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>ers, ev</sub>id<sub>ence c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>ver</sub>ifi<sub>er-</sub>b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay.</sub> R<sub>ecor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>s</sub> en<sub>g</sub>ines and retain available librar<sub>y p</sub>rovenance. CUADebu<sub>g</sub> (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026b) studies dia<sub>g</sub>nosis and re<sub>p</sub>air for <sub>screens</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>er-use</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s;</sub> <sub>our</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>-on</sub>l<sub>y.</sub>

These checks test the environment <sub>p</sub>orts before their out<sub>p</sub>uts are used as failure labels; the<sub>y</sub> do <sub>no</sub>t <sub>assess</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>b<sub>ugger</sub>’<sub>s exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

## D.1. Construction algorithm

<table><tr><td colspan="2">AET stage contracts</td></tr><tr><td>Stage</td><td>Stored information and admission boundary</td></tr><tr><td>1. Collect</td><td>Adapters supply tasks, instructions, tools and success verifiers. Save policy, harness and environment identities, acting units, actions, observations and final outcomes, including successes. Failures arise naturally; we do not inject them. Preserve service outages, harness failures and unusable graders for audits, but exclude them from agent-error targets. Collection requires no replay support.</td></tr><tr><td>2. Diagnose</td><td>Following AgentDebug (Zhu et al., 2025), propose an error step, responsible agent or component, explanation with trace quotations, and replacement action. Record privileged reference or verifier information separately from learner inputs. Where supported, replay feedback guides revisions within a fixed budget. Keep every attempt and distinguish the first proposal from one selected after retries. Multiple debuggers can reuse a stored failure.</td></tr><tr><td>3. Ground</td><td>Resolve cited steps, acting units and quotations in the exported student input. Check tool validity and information availability. Semantic judges assess the attribution and repair; both judges must accept in the dual-judge subset (Appendix E.6). Save accepted and rejected decisions. These are machine judgments; human validity and training eligibility require separate assessment.</td></tr><tr><td>4. Replay</td><td>Start two new executions from a restored or reconstructed checkpoint: one substitutes the correction and one repeats the original action. Match policy, harness, budget, verifier and temperature schedule; save seeds, sample counts, checkpoint fidelity and both outcomes, including failed repairs and passing controls. The failed source rollout does not serve as this control. Recovery supports the correction in that setting, not necessarily its explanation or a unique root cause. Coached retries use a separate protocol.</td></tr><tr><td>5. Build views</td><td>Diagnosis targets use supported attributions without requiring replay. Recovery targets use an executed, passing continuation and preserve its branch identity. Only eligible continuation actions receive loss; input history, tool observations and rejected continuation actions receive no loss. Preferences also require a valid action comparison under the same visible input. Source-task grouping, declared splits and content checks determine training eligibility separately from label quality. Retain records excluded from a view and their selection history. Derive evidence grades from stored outcomes.</td></tr></table>

Failure outcomes and attributable errors. Adapters evaluate task completion using answer or result matching, <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>s, or goa</sub>l<sub>-s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h <sub>a recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>nega</sub>ti<sub>ve ou</sub>t<sub>come</sub> f<sub>rom a va</sub>lid t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>-</sub>f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure ver</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>rom a suppor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>
<table><tr><td colspan="2">Failure boundaries</td></tr><tr><td>Observed case</td><td>Interpretation and admission rule</td></tr><tr><td>Wrong answer, failed tests or unmet goal</td><td>A valid negative task verdict supplies a diagnosis candidate; it does not identify the erroneous decision.</td></tr><tr><td>Tool error followed by task success</td><td>Exclude from the failed-run cohort. An intermediate exception need not cause task failure.</td></tr><tr><td>Budget exhaustion, loop or early stop</td><td>A candidate under a defensible execution budget. Inspect where progress broke down; do not blame the cutoff step by default.</td></tr><tr><td>Invalid tool action</td><td>A candidate if the agent had a valid tool contract. Schema bugs, service outages and environment crashes require separate accounting.</td></tr><tr><td>No usable verifier verdict</td><td>Quarantine for agent-error supervision. An unavailable reference or grader exception cannot establish task failure.</td></tr><tr><td>Recorded-page action mismatch</td><td>Establishes deviation from the recorded path, not inability to recover in a live browser; exclude from strict admission.</td></tr></table>

For attribution, reviewers seek the earliest supported consequential mistake with a feasible alternative, not a later <sub>symp</sub>t<sub>om or a un</sub>i<sub>que g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l <sub>roo</sub>t <sub>cause.</sub> Th<sub>ey can use</sub> th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on as re</sub>t<sub>rospec</sub>ti<sub>ve ev</sub>id<sub>ence;</sub> th<sub>e propose</sub>d action must res<sub>p</sub>ect information available before execution (A<sub>pp</sub>endix E.6). Unsu<sub>pp</sub>orted attributions fail semantic <sub>a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>on.</sub>

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure</sub> fl<sub>ags,</sub> <sub>source</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k<sub>age,</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> <sub>presence</sub> <sub>an</sub>d t<sub>race</sub> <sub>quo</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>screen</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>recogn</sub>i<sub>zes</sub> k<sub>nown</sub> <sub>non-agen</sub>t <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>t<sub>ures;</sub> it d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>cover</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>er-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> <sub>ungra</sub>d<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come.</sub> Th<sub>us</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on coun</sub>t<sub>s</sub> d<sub>o no</sub>t <sub>cer</sub>tif <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c va</sub>lidit <sub>or</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>n e</sub>li ibilit <sub>.</sub> R<sub>ecover</sub> t<sub>ar e</sub>t<sub>s a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>ll <sub>re u</sub>i<sub>re</sub> <sub>an execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, pass</sub>i<sub>ng con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

## D.2. Evidence ladder and statistical claims

<table><tr><td>Evidence state</td><td>Required observation</td></tr><tr><td>Ungrounded</td><td>Citation support is absent, regardless of replay outcome.</td></tr><tr><td>EO</td><td>Grounded diagnosis; replay was not attempted and no checkpoint was named.</td></tr><tr><td>No / partial recovery</td><td>Executed treatment never succeeds / has mixed outcomes. A named but unexecuted checkpoint also receives the legacy no-recovery label; typed replay status distinguishes it from a failed trial.</td></tr><tr><td>E2</td><td>All treatment trials pass, all controls fail, at least two trials per arm, and both arms complete their planned counts.</td></tr></table>

D.3. Lineage and checkpoint fidelity
<table><tr><td>Check</td><td>Requirement and interpretation</td></tr><tr><td>Branch identity</td><td>Recompute the v1 attestation of source trace, pre-action prompt, restored state, trial, applied action and actor transcript.</td></tr><tr><td>Native messages</td><td>Match tool-call identifiers and result ordering. Keep reconstructed external logs in a separate fidelity stratum.</td></tr><tr><td>Action authorship</td><td>Attribute an action to the judge only for substitution. A policy&#x27;s coached continuation is not the judge&#x27;s proposed action.</td></tr><tr><td>Exclusions</td><td>Reject a training package when invalid-lineage exclusions exceed the implemented 5% ceiling. This controls attrition, not semantic accuracy</td></tr><tr><td>Family references</td><td>An imported ALFWorld family match is not an executed success on the target instance. The probe-to-judge prompt still needs family-scope propagation; keep those results separate from exact-task reference effects</td></tr></table>

## E.6. Quality rubric and certification

The automated rubric has twelve items. A row must pass all except item 10, which records ambiguity without rejecting the row. Deterministic checks decide items 4 and 11; Claude O<sub>p</sub>us 5 and Gemini 3.1 Pro (<sub>p</sub>review) jud<sub>g</sub>e the remainin<sub>g</sub> items after those checks. Both judges must accept each rejecting item. The human form is shorter (Appendix W).

<table><tr><td colspan="3">Admission rubric: what must hold</td></tr><tr><td>Item</td><td>Check</td><td>Accept when; reject otherwise</td></tr><tr><td>1</td><td>Agent failure</td><td>An available agent decision could have avoided failure; exclude infrastructure, harness and grader faults. Budget exhaustion under a reasonable budget can be an agent failure.</td></tr><tr><td>2</td><td>Attributed step</td><td>The trace supports a mistake at the named step, with no earlier supported decisive mistake. A final stop or a later symptom is insufficient.</td></tr><tr><td>3</td><td>Responsible unit</td><td>The named unit appears in the trace and made the decision.</td></tr><tr><td>4</td><td>Quotations</td><td>Every quote resolves verbatim in the student input at or before the blamed step; a truncation marker is not evidence.</td></tr><tr><td>5</td><td>Entailment</td><td>The visible evidence supports the explanation&#x27;s factual claims.</td></tr><tr><td>6</td><td>Information access</td><td>The diagnosis uses visible evidence; the repair uses information available before the action, not its result or a future answer.</td></tr><tr><td>7</td><td>Executability</td><td>The tool exists, arguments satisfy its schema, and the action can be issued at that point without placeholders.</td></tr><tr><td>8</td><td>Corrective change</td><td>The proposal changes the blamed action and addresses the stated mistake.</td></tr><tr><td>9</td><td>Specificity</td><td>The explanation names a concrete action, contradicted fact and consequence.</td></tr><tr><td>10</td><td>Ambiguity</td><td>Record defensible alternatives. This item does not reject the row.</td></tr><tr><td>11</td><td>Leakage and secrets</td><td>No unseen gold answer, verifier answer or credential survives in the student input or target.</td></tr><tr><td>12</td><td>Input fidelity</td><td>Preserve the received task, instructions, tools and decision order; mark omissions and parallel calls.</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td colspan="2">Mechanical checks and export handling</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2">Check family</td></tr><tr><td>Structure and visibility</td><td>Implementation boundary Require an admitted attempt, an asserted error, a visible step and matching answer fields.</td></tr><tr><td>Environment validity</td><td>Missing required records fail closed. Preserve malformed turns and parallel-call notes. Reject retrieval outages, task quarantine, gold-grader contradictions, harness-submitted actions and known prompt/tool-contract defects. Exclude static Mind2Web from this</td></tr><tr><td>Evidence and leakage</td><td>admission protocol. Require a quote from before the blamed step; reject future-only evidence, copied gold</td></tr><tr><td>Repair and attribution</td><td>actions, verifier text and arguments based on hidden content. Validate tool arguments and changed action; reject later actions copied back as repairs.</td></tr><tr><td>Export</td><td>Require a unit that acted and sufficient voter support. Restore policy instructions and tool lists, align answer fields, re-derive the unit and redact credentials on both sides. Log each transformation.</td></tr></table>

Rubric v5 also rejects later parallel calls labeled without turn-order notes and quotes that were unavailable when the action was c<sup>h</sup>osen. Among 69 disagreements on re-admitted rows, a <sup>h</sup>and c<sup>h</sup>ec<sup>k</sup> o<sup>f</sup> 10 <sup>f</sup>ound t<sup>h</sup>e stricter judge correct on 8; 4 o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>ose invo<sup>l</sup>ved unavai<sup>l</sup>a<sup>bl</sup>e para<sup>ll</sup>e<sup>l</sup>-ca<sup>ll</sup> resu<sup>l</sup>ts. T<sup>h</sup>e ot<sup>h</sup>er judge was correct on 2. T<sup>h</sup>is targeted c<sup>h</sup>ec<sup>k</sup> is not a popu<sup>l</sup>ation accuracy estimate. T<sup>h</sup>e 98-case eva<sup>l</sup>uation su<sup>b</sup>set remains <sup>f</sup>rozen under v2; v5 does not rede<sup>fi</sup>ne it. V<sub>ers</sub>i<sub>one</sub>d <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll f<sub>a</sub>il d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>ons an</sub>d <sub>or</sub>d<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>t<sub>ures rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e so</sub>ft<sub>ware re</sub>l<sub>ease.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ave no</sub>t <sub>measure</sub>d <sub>cer</sub>tifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on ra</sub>t<sub>es</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e correc</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t <sub>poo</sub>l <sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

SET Attendance = &#x27;1,500&#x27; 2 Commit final answer 1,500</td><td>Row includes 1,458. [] Final answer committed.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2">Recorded target Blame step 2 for committing without a verification query. Reading The absence of a second SELECT does not show that the UPDATE failed. Inspect the post-update state and grader before accepting this diagnosis; the empty SQL result alone does not settle it. Source Purposively selected historical audit case; no independent adjudication. This example illustrates a label limitation.</td></tr></table>

## G. Taxonomy definitions and validation study

Taxonomy scope. ## I.5. Comparison scope and interpretation

## I.13. Compact-target training-set size

Size. If the full cohort does not help, does a smaller one, and what does the student learn as the training set grows?

Nested su<sup>b</sup>sets o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e sca<sup>l</sup>e arm, cut to w<sup>h</sup>o<sup>l</sup>e optimizer windows o<sup>f</sup> 12 source tas<sup>k</sup>s (120, 252, 504 and 1,020 tas<sup>k</sup>s, <sup>h</sup>ence not powers o<sup>f</sup> two), are eac<sup>h</sup> trained wit<sup>h</sup> one seed under t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> arm’s recipe; t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> 1,656-tas<sup>k</sup> arm contri<sup>b</sup>utes t<sup>h</sup>e t<sup>h</sup>ree seeds o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e main run, and a<sup>ll fi</sup>ve points are scored on t<sup>h</sup>e same 943 <sup>h</sup>e<sup>l</sup>d-out cases. Pu<sup>bl</sup>ic-sp<sup>l</sup>it resu<sup>l</sup>ts exist on<sup>l</sup>y <sup>f</sup>or t<sup>h</sup>e sma<sup>ll</sup>est su<sup>b</sup>set and t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> arm. T<sup>h</sup>e su<sup>b</sup>sets reac<sup>h</sup> 48.89, 47.72, 47.40 and 46.13 micro exact step against 47.19 <sup>f</sup>or t<sup>h</sup>e untrained <sup>b</sup>ase and 46.59 ± 0.12 <sup>f</sup>or t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> arm (Figure 14). Accuracy is <sup>l</sup>ower at <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> sca<sup>l</sup>e t<sup>h</sup>an at t<sup>h</sup>e sma<sup>ll</sup>est su<sup>b</sup>set; at <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> size seed 17 sits 0.53 <sup>b</sup>e<sup>l</sup>ow t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>b</sup>ase and t<sup>h</sup>e t<sup>h</sup>ree-seed mean 0.60. ## K. Sensitivity Analyses

## K.1. Multi-round repair yield and diagnosis changes

What changes across repair rounds? A later diagnosis need not identify a later error. Across 1,744 transitions in 405 mu<sup>l</sup>ti-round attempts, 1,064 (61.0%) name t<sup>h</sup>e same step again, 363 (20.8%) move <sup>l</sup>ater, and 317 (18.2%) move earlier (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 16a, A<sub>pp</sub>endix K). ## L. Error reporting on successful trajectories

On 50 success<sup>f</sup>u<sup>l</sup> trajectories, GPT-5 mini, GPT-5.6 So<sup>l</sup> and C<sup>l</sup>aude Opus 5 eac<sup>h</sup> name an error under a prompt t<sup>h</sup>at presupposes <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>ure: none o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e 150 primed cases a<sup>b</sup>stains. Removing t<sup>h</sup>at premise and a<sup>ll</sup>owing “none” yie<sup>l</sup>ds a<sup>b</sup>stention rates o<sup>f</sup> 84%, 86%, and 44% (Figure 16<sup>b</sup>). T<sup>h</sup>e <sup>fi</sup>rst two mode<sup>l</sup>s sti<sup>ll</sup> report errors on 10 o<sup>f</sup> 10 matc<sup>h</sup>ed <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>ures. T<sup>h</sup>ese are error-reporting rates, not automatica<sup>ll</sup>y <sup>f</sup>a<sup>l</sup>se-positive rates: 39 o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e 50 successes contain a <sub>recovera</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>ccup.</sub>

We used paired prompts on the same trajectories: the AgentErrorBench instruction verbatim and a version that removes th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure prem</sub>i<sub>se an</sub>d <sub>perm</sub>it<sub>s</sub> “<sub>none</sub>”<sub>.</sub> R<sub>en</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> t<sub>runca</sub>ti<sub>on ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h th<sub>e ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> sample spans twelve environments, seven harnesses and ten policies, with one trajectory per task. Attribution accuracy still requires semantic adjudication

$$

$$

Construction | Panel system instruction   
You are an expert debugger of LLM agents. You will read one failed agent trajectory and produce a   
failure diagnosis. Be precise about the DECISIVE error: the earliest step whose correction would   
have turned the run into a success -- not the step where the failure became visible.

Th<sub>e pane</sub>l’<sub>s user message supp</sub>li<sub>es can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or ar</sub>bit<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> E<sub>v</sub>id<sub>ence mus</sub>t <sub>quo</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bl<sub>ame</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>an</sub> <sub>ear</sub>li<sub>er cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure</sub> t<sub>o exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n;</sub> it i<sub>s no</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>e ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> k<sub>new.</sub> R<sub>e</sub>f<sub>erence-ass</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>ons rema</sub>i<sub>n separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

Construction | Solo attribution and replacement   
You are an AI assistant tasked with analyzing agent conversation history when solving a real world   
problem.   
Respond ONLY with a JSON object matching this schema (no prose, no markdown):   
{   
"span\_id": "<event\_id from the input or null>",   
"step\_index": <int or null>,   
"agent\_name": "<agent\_name from the input or null>",   
"confidence": <float between 0 and 1>,   
"rationale": "<one or two sentences justifying the choice>",   
"evidence": ["<short quoted evidence>", ...],   
"corrected\_action": {"tool": "<tool name>", "args": {<arguments>}} or null   
}   
If the trajectory does not appear to have failed, return all fields as null and   
confidence 0.   
"corrected\_action" is the ONE concrete action that should have replaced the blamed step's   
action, as {"tool": "<tool name>", "args": {<arguments>}}.   
Rules for it:   
1. Use the tool names and argument keys the trajectory itself uses. Do not invent a tool the   
agent had no access to.   
2. "args" must be a JSON object.   
3. Return null unless you can name a specific tool and its arguments. A placeholder, a   
paraphrase of your rationale, or a guess is WORSE than null.   
4. Return null when the blamed step is not an action at all (a plan, a reflection, an   
observation), and when the step's own action was already correct.

You are an expert debugger of LLM agents. You will read one agent trajectory that failed its task and identify the recorded, evidence-grounded failure attribution. Answer with one JSON object and nothing else, with exactly these keys in this order: decisive\_step, responsible\_agent, rationale. \`decisive\_step\` is an integer step index shown in the transcript, \`responsible\_agent\` a string naming a unit that appears in the transcript, and \`rationale\` one sentence stating the mistake at that step in terms of what the transcript shows.

You are an expert debugger of LLM agents. You will read one agent trajectory that failed its task and predict one recorded, evidence-grounded failure attribution and any proposed correction in the record. The correction may have failed replay or replay may have been unavailable, so this target is not evidence of a recovering or causally effective action. It is also not guaranteed to be

the earliest error, the root cause, or a unique decisive step. Answer with one JSON object and   
nothing else, with exactly these keys in this order: has\_error, decisive\_step, responsible\_agent,   
alternative\_steps, error\_explanation, evidence, corrected\_action. \`has\_error\` is a boolean,   
decisive\_step\` an integer step index shown in the transcript, \`responsible\_agent\` a string naming   
a unit that appears in the transcript, \`alternative\_steps\` a list of integers,   
error\_explanation\` a string, \`evidence\` a list of strings quoted verbatim from the transcript,   
and \`corrected\_action\` either an object or null.

O<sub>n</sub>l<sub>y ass</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t t<sub>arge</sub>t t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> l<sub>oss.</sub> A <sub>recovery-or</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>expor</sub>t h<sub>as a separa</sub>t<sub>e execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ranc</sub>h <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>remen</sub>t<sub>;</sub> it <sub>mus</sub>t <sub>no</sub>t <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ace</sub> th<sub>e compac</sub>t i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>h<sub>en repro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

## U.1. Native-context public evaluation

Who&When | Exact system instruction   
You are given the transcript of a failed multi-agent run. Identify the single step where the decisive   
mistake was made, and which agent made it.   
Reason inside <think></think>, then give the final answer inside <answer></answer> as \`agentID |   
stepID\`, where stepID is the integer step index shown in the transcript.   
Answer with exactly that format and nothing after </answer>.   
User sufix: Which step and which agent?

AgentErrorBench | Exact system instruction   
You are given the transcript of a failed single-agent run, rendered as numbered agent steps; each   
step holds the environment message the agent received and the agent's reply. Identify the single   
numbered step where the critical failure occurred and classify its failure module.   
Reason inside <think></think>, then give the final answer inside <answer></answer> as \`module |   
stepID\`, where the module is one of action, plan, planning, memory, reflection, or system and   
stepID is the integer step index shown in the transcript.   
Answer with exactly that format and nothing after </answer>.   
User sufix: Which step and which failure module?

TrajErrBench | Exact system instruction   
You are given a failed long-horizon agent trajectory. Identify the single numbered step where the   
earliest critical failure occurred and classify its error module.   
Reason inside <think></think>, then give the final answer inside <answer></answer> as \`module |   
stepID\`, where module is one of act, obs, plan, reason, or verify and stepID is the integer step   
index shown in the trajectory.   
Answer with exactly that format and nothing after </answer>.   
User sufix: Which step and which error module?

## W.2. Sample, interface and instructions

Sample and assignment. Four raters, identified only as A–D, each received 40 records; every record was assigned to two raters (80 distinct records, 160 assignments). T<sup>h</sup>e raters were paper aut<sup>h</sup>ors wit<sup>h</sup> doctora<sup>l</sup> or AI/LLM researc<sup>h</sup> <sup>b</sup>ac<sup>k</sup>grounds and participated vo<sup>l</sup>untari<sup>l</sup>y. T<sup>h</sup>e samp<sup>l</sup>e contains 48 <sup>fil</sup>ter survivors, 16 judge-rejected records and 16 gate-rejected records <sup>f</sup>rom a <sup>h</sup>istorica<sup>l</sup> co<sup>ll</sup>ection <sup>f</sup>rame. It spans 30 environments, inc<sup>l</sup>uding 31 records outside t<sup>h</sup>e re<sup>l</sup>eased core. T<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>rame contains 28,432 rows; t<sup>h</sup>e 40,000-c<sup>h</sup>aracter reading cap exc<sup>l</sup>udes 1,255 rows. Samp<sup>l</sup>ing retains one record per source tas<sup>k</sup> and trace. Pair over<sup>l</sup>aps are AB=14, AC=13, AD=13, BC=13, BD=13 and CD=14.
